with the development of data collections included in
such sites. Your institution may already be a subscriber.
Follow the links above to find out more about the data
in these sites and their terms of usage.

<h3>Gas Phase Heat Capacity (Shomate Equation)</h3>
<p>
C<sub>p</sub>&deg; = A + B*t + C*t<sup>2</sup> + D*t<sup>3</sup> +
 E/t<sup>2</sup><br />
H&deg; &#8722; H&deg;<sub>298.15</sub>= A*t + B*t<sup>2</sup>/2 +
 C*t<sup>3</sup>/3 + D*t<sup>4</sup>/4 &#8722; E/t + F &#8722; H<br />
S&deg; = A*ln(t) + B*t + C*t<sup>2</sup>/2 + D*t<sup>3</sup>/3 &#8722;
 E/(2*t<sup>2</sup>) + G<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; C<sub>p</sub> = heat capacity (J/mol*K)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; H&deg; = standard enthalpy (kJ/mol)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; S&deg; = standard entropy (J/mol*K)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; t = temperature (K) / 1000.<br />
</p>
<p>
<a href="/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C7553562&amp;Mask=FFFF&amp;Type=JANAFG&amp;Plot=on#JANAFG">View plot</a>
Requires a JavaScript / HTML 5 canvas capable browser.
</p>
<p>
<a href="/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C7553562&amp;Mask=FFFF&amp;Type=JANAFG&amp;Table=on#JANAFG">View table</a>.
</p>
<table class="data" aria-label="Gas Phase Heat Capacity (Shomate Equation)" style="width: 50.00%;">

</colgroup>

<h3>Liquid Phase Heat Capacity (Shomate Equation)</h3>
<p>
C<sub>p</sub>&deg; = A + B*t + C*t<sup>2</sup> + D*t<sup>3</sup> +
 E/t<sup>2</sup><br />
H&deg; &#8722; H&deg;<sub>298.15</sub>= A*t + B*t<sup>2</sup>/2 +
 C*t<sup>3</sup>/3 + D*t<sup>4</sup>/4 &#8722; E/t + F &#8722; H<br />
S&deg; = A*ln(t) + B*t + C*t<sup>2</sup>/2 + D*t<sup>3</sup>/3 &#8722;
 E/(2*t<sup>2</sup>) + G<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; C<sub>p</sub> = heat capacity (J/mol*K)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; H&deg; = standard enthalpy (kJ/mol)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; S&deg; = standard entropy (J/mol*K)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; t = temperature (K) / 1000.<br />
</p>
<p>
<a href="/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C7553562&amp;Mask=FFFF&amp;Type=JANAFL&amp;Plot=on#JANAFL">View plot</a>
Requires a JavaScript / HTML 5 canvas capable browser.
</p>
<p>
<a href="/cgi/cbook.cgi?ID=C7553562&amp;Mask=FFFF&amp;Type=JANAFL&amp;Table=on#JANAFL">View table</a>.
</p>

<h3>Solid Phase Heat Capacity (Shomate Equation)</h3>
<p>
C<sub>p</sub>&deg; = A + B*t + C*t<sup>2</sup> + D*t<sup>3</sup> +
 E/t<sup>2</sup><br />
H&deg; &#8722; H&deg;<sub>298.15</sub>= A*t + B*t<sup>2</sup>/2 +
 C*t<sup>3</sup>/3 + D*t<sup>4</sup>/4 &#8722; E/t + F &#8722; H<br />
S&deg; = A*ln(t) + B*t + C*t<sup>2</sup>/2 + D*t<sup>3</sup>/3 &#8722;
 E/(2*t<sup>2</sup>) + G<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; C<sub>p</sub> = heat capacity (J/mol*K)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; H&deg; = standard enthalpy (kJ/mol)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; S&deg; = standard entropy (J/mol*K)<br />
&nbsp; &nbsp; t = temperature (K) / 1000.<br />
</p>

<p class="section-head"><strong>Go To:</strong> <a href="#Top">Top</a>, <a href="#Thermo-Gas">Gas phase thermochemistry data</a>, <a href="#Thermo-Condensed">Condensed phase thermochemistry data</a>, <a href="#Thermo-Phase">Phase change data</a>, <a href="#Thermo-React">Reaction thermochemistry data</a>, <a href="#Ion-Energetics">Gas phase ion energetics data</a>, <a href="#Ion-Cluster">Ion clustering data</a>, <a href="#Diatomic">Constants of diatomic molecules</a>, <a href="#NIST-Free-Links">NIST Free Links</a>, <a href="#Refs">References</a>, <a href="#Notes">Notes</a></p>

<h3>Henry's Law constant (water solution)</h3>
<p style="margin-left: 2em; text-indent: -2em">
k<sub>H</sub>(T) = <strong>k&deg;<sub>H</sub></strong> exp(<strong><em>d</em>(ln(k<sub>H</sub>))/<em>d</em>(1/T)</strong> ((1/T) - 1/(298.15 K)))<br />
<strong>k&deg;<sub>H</sub></strong> = Henry's law constant for solubility in water at 298.15 K (mol/(kg*bar))<br />
<strong><em>d</em>(ln(k<sub>H</sub>))/<em>d</em>(1/T)</strong> = Temperature dependence constant (K)
</p>
<table class="data" aria-label="Henry's Law constant (water solution)"><tr>
<th scope="col">k&deg;<sub>H</sub> (mol/(kg*bar))</th>
<th scope="col">d(ln(k<sub>H</sub>))/d(1/T) (K)</th>
<th scope="col">Method</th>
<th scope="col">Reference</th>
<th scope="col">Comment</th>
</tr>
<tr class="exp"><td class="right-nowrap">3.0</td><td class="right-nowrap">4400.</td><td style="text-align: center;"><a href="/chemistry/solubili.html#R">R</a></td><td style="text-align: left;">N/A</td><td style="text-align: left;">&nbsp;</td></tr>
<tr class="exp"><td class="right-nowrap">1.1</td><td class="right-nowrap">&nbsp;</td><td style="text-align: center;"><a href="/chemistry/solubili.html#C">C</a></td><td style="text-align: left;">N/A</td><td style="text-align: left;"> <i>missing citation</i> quote a paper as the source that gives only the solubility but not the Henry's law constant.</td></tr>
<tr class="exp"><td class="right-nowrap">3.3</td><td class="right-nowrap">4800.</td><td style="text-align: center;"><a href="/chemistry/solubili.html#T">T</a></td><td style="text-align: left;">N/A</td><td style="text-align: left;">&nbsp;</td></tr>
<tr class="exp"><td class="right-nowrap">3.1</td><td class="right-nowrap">4600.</td><td style="text-align: center;"><a href="/chemistry/solubili.html#R">R</a></td><td style="text-align: left;">N/A</td><td style="text-align: left;">&nbsp;</td></tr>

<tr>
<td colspan="13">The absorption spectrum from 450000 to 870000 cm<sup>-1</sup> (55.8 to 107.9 eV)
at low resolution has been described by  <a href="#ref-65">Myer and Samson, 1970</a>. It corresponds to excitation from 4d shell
to various unfilled orbitals.</td></tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="13">&#8627;<a href="#ref-70">Comes, Nielsen, et al., 1973</a></td></tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="13">The absorption spectrum in the VUV region at high resolution has most recently
been photographed by  <a href="#ref-64">Venkateswarlu, 1970</a> who gives an extensive table of observed features in the region
56500 - 75800 cm<sup>-1</sup>. Most of the bands are assigned to extended Rydberg series
converging to a common limit at 75814 cm<sup>-1</sup> (9.400 eV), a smaller number to fragments
of series converging to 80895 cm<sup>-1</sup> (10.03 eV). The limits are assumed to correspond to
v=0 of X <sup>2</sup> &#928;<sub>g</sub>, 3/2 and 1/2, respectively, of I<sub>2</sub><sup>+</sup>; see, however, <a href="#Dia1">1</a>. Several of the
progressions observed in absorption  <a href="#ref-64">Venkateswarlu, 1970</a> appear to correspond to emission bands recorded
by  <a href="#ref-71">Haranath and Rao, 1958</a> under medium resolution in the region 56000 - 68000 cm<sup>-1</sup> and classified by them
as belonging to twelve systems. See also  <a href="#ref-72">Cordes, 1935</a>  <a href="#ref-65">Myer and Samson, 1970</a>*.</td></tr>

<h3>Notes</h3>

<table role="presentation" aria-label="List of Notes">
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia1">1</a></td><td>From the photoelectron spectrum  <a href="#ref-62">Potts and Price, 1971</a>,  <a href="#ref-61">Higginson, Lloyd, et al., 1973</a>; adiabatic potential established by
temperature variation. The same method yields 9.953 eV for the ionization potential to
<sup>2</sup>&#928;<sub>1/2g</sub>(v=0). Neither result agrees with the values obtained by  <a href="#ref-64">Venkateswarlu, 1970</a> from Rydberg series,
i.e. 9.400 and 10.03 eV. The discrepancy could be understood if the Rydberg series were to
correspond to v'=3, but the absence of series with v' = 0, 1, 2 would still be puzzling.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia2">2</a></td><td>Weak emission bands in the presence of foreign gases, 2785-2731 . T<sub>e</sub> is based on the
assumption that the lower state is the B state, but  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>, has suggested that instead it may be the D
state leading to T<sub>e</sub> ~ 76872 cm<sup>-1</sup>  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia3">3</a></td><td>Strong emission bands in the presence of foreign gases, 3460 - 3015 Angstroms. It is by no
means certain that the lower state has been correctly identified as the B state.  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>, suggests that
the bands arise from the transition G &#8594; A'.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia4">4</a></td><td>From the intensity distribution and Franck-Condon principle  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>,  <a href="#ref-78">Wieland, Tellinghuisen, et al., 1972</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia5">5</a></td><td>In emission in electric discharges in the presence of foreign gases, 2740 - 2490 . Also
observed for <sup>129</sup> I<sub>2</sub>, confirming the vibrational numbering. Emission bands in the region
2240 - 1950 Angstroms are assigned by  <a href="#ref-71">Haranath and Rao, 1958</a>, to a separate system (called H &#8594; X), with
v<sub>00</sub> = 48072 and w ~ 79. It seems, however, possible that these bands belong to F &#8594; X.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia6">6</a></td><td>Configuration...&#963;<sub>g</sub><sup>2</sup>&#960;<sub>u</sub><sup>3</sup>&#960;<sub>g</sub><sup>3</sup>&#963;<sub>u</sub><sup>2</sup>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia7">7</a></td><td>The analysis of this fairly extensive system [2400 - 2240 , called E &#8594; X by  <a href="#ref-71">Haranath and Rao, 1958</a>] is not
yet supported by isotope studies, nor is it seen in absorption.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia8">8</a></td><td>Suggested upper state of high temperature absorption &quot;continuum&quot;
shortward of 3263 (30640 cm<sup>-1</sup>)</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia9">9</a></td><td>Configuration...&#963;<sub>g</sub>&#960;<sub>u</sub><sup>4</sup>&#960;<sub>g</sub><sup>3</sup>&#963;<sub>u</sub><sup>2</sup>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia10">10</a></td><td>Emission bands in the presence of foreign gases, 4400 - 4000 . Also observed for <sup>129</sup>I<sub>2</sub>,
confirming the vibrational numbering. The E &#8594; B fluorescence spectrum following
two-photon absorption  <a href="#ref-89">Rousseau and Williams, 1974</a>, consists of transitions both to the discrete and to the continuous part
of B, the latter giving rise to diffuse bands (&quot;structured&quot; continuum)  <a href="#ref-90">Tellinghuisen, 1975</a>. From a comparison of
the calculated with the observed intensity distribution  <a href="#ref-90">Tellinghuisen, 1975</a>, obtains the potential function of E as
well as the variation of the transition moment with r. The lifetime of E &#8594; B is 27 ns  <a href="#ref-91">Rousseau, 1975</a>
confirming that this is an allowed transition and that the E state is <sup>3</sup>&#928;<sub>0+g</sub>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia11">11</a></td><td>The v' numbering is uncertain and, therefore, the vibrational constants are subject to change.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia12">12</a></td><td>The system includes the absorption bands of  <a href="#ref-92">Pringsheim and Rosen, 1928</a>,  <a href="#ref-93">Kimura and Miyanishi, 1929</a>,  <a href="#ref-72">Cordes, 1935</a>, remeasured by  <a href="#ref-94">Nobs and Wieland, 1966</a>. It also includes
the resonance series of  <a href="#ref-95">Verma, 1960</a> in the region 1830 - 2370 which arise from very high vibrational
levels (v' ~ 195), of the D state excited by the 1830 atomic line of iodine. The system further
includes the diffuse emission bands in the region 2500 - 5000 with a characteristic group near
3250 [McLennan bands  <a href="#ref-96">McLennan, 1913</a>]. The diffuse bands have been recognized by  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>, to correspond to
transitions from D to the continuum of X [Condon diffraction bands, see also  <a href="#ref-97">Tellinghuisen, 1974</a>]. Earlier
summaries  <a href="#ref-80">Mathieson and Rees, 1956</a>,  <a href="#ref-71">Haranath and Rao, 1958</a>,  <a href="#ref-64">Venkateswarlu, 1970</a> gave an electronic state at T<sub>e</sub> = 51427.9 with &#969;<sub>e</sub> = 169.41, &#969;<sub>e</sub>x<sub>e</sub>
= 0.941, &#969;<sub>e</sub>y<sub>e</sub> = +0.0022 which was to represent the Cordes absorption bands from 1950 to
1795  <a href="#ref-72">Cordes, 1935</a>. Following  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>,  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>, we consider these bands as part of D &#8592; X.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia13">13</a></td><td>Configuration . . . &#963;<sub>g</sub>&#960;<sub>u</sub><sup>4</sup>&#960;<sub>g</sub><sup>4</sup>&#963;<sub>u</sub>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia14">14</a></td><td>Suggested upper state of high temperature absorption &quot;continuum&quot;
shortward of 3427 (29170 cm<sup>-1</sup>)</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia15">15</a></td><td>The G &#8594;A' transition has been observed to lase strongly when mixtures of HI or CF<sub>3</sub>I
or CH<sub>3</sub>I with argon (1000 - 4000 torr), are excited by a pulsed high current electron beam  <a href="#ref-98">Hays, Hoffman, et al., 1976</a>.
See also H &#8594; B footnote <a href="#Dia3">3</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia16">16</a></td><td>Repulsive state from <sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub> + <sup>2</sup>P<sub>1/2</sub> responsible for a weak but
broad absorption continuum with maximum at 2700 (37000 cm<sup>-1</sup>). <a href="#Dia38">38</a></td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia17">17</a></td><td>Repulsive state from <sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub> + <sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub>, responsible for absorption
continuum with maximum at 20050 cm<sup>-1</sup> and for the predissociation of B <sup>3</sup>&#928;<sub>0+u</sub>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia18">18</a></td><td>f values based on magnetic circular dichroism spectra have been estimated as 0.0018
(B&quot;&#8592;X) and 0.009 (B &#8592;X) and have been compared with earlier results  <a href="#ref-99">Brith, Rowe, et al., 1975</a>. For a
comparison of theoretical and observed intensities in the B &#8594; X resonance series see  <a href="#ref-100">Zare, 1964</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia19">19</a></td><td>Configuration . . . &#963;<sub>g</sub><sup>2</sup>&#960;<sub>u</sub><sup>4</sup>&#960;<sub>g</sub><sup>3</sup>&#963;<sub>u</sub>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia20">20</a></td><td>Repulsive state from <sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub> + <sup>2</sup>P<sub>3/2</sub>. The previous assignment of B'
as the state responsible for the magnetic field induced predissociation of B is now in doubt;
See <a href="#Dia23">23</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia21">21</a></td><td>-0.00178(v +1/2)<sup>3</sup> - 0.0000738(v+1/2)<sup>4</sup> + 0.00000103(v+1/2)<sup>5</sup>, from levels with
4&#8804;v&#8804;50  <a href="#ref-84">Barrow and Yee, 1973</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia22">22</a></td><td>Somewhat different constants, valid for 4&#8804;v&#8804;77, are given by  <a href="#ref-85">Wei and Tellinghuisen, 1974</a>, T<sub>e</sub> = 15768.32, &#969;<sub>e</sub> =
126.165, &#969;<sub>e</sub>x<sub>e</sub> = 0.8673, ..., B<sub>e</sub> = 0.028939, &#945;<sub>e</sub> = 0.0001204, ... (using calculated
D<sub>v</sub> values); see also  <a href="#ref-101">Brown, Burns, et al., 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-102">Tellinghuisen, 1976</a>. RKR potential curve  <a href="#ref-84">Barrow and Yee, 1973</a>. For a discussion of the long-range
potential and &#916;G, B<sub>v</sub> values near the dissociation limit
see  <a href="#ref-103">LeRoy and Bernstein, 1971</a>,  <a href="#ref-104">LeRoy, 1972</a>,  <a href="#ref-84">Barrow and Yee, 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-105">Le Roy, 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-106">Yee, 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-107">LeRoy, 1974</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia23">23</a></td><td>Collision induced predissociation of the B state  <i>missing citation</i>; magnetic field induced
predissociation  <a href="#ref-108">Degenkolb, Steinfeld, et al., 1969</a>,  <a href="#ref-109">Capelle and Broida, 1972</a>,  <a href="#ref-110">Chapman and Bunker, 1972</a>; spontaneous predissociation  <a href="#ref-111">Tellinghuisen, 1972</a>; hyperfine predissociation  <a href="#ref-112">Broyer, Vigue, et al., 1976</a>.
The purely radiative lifetime  <a href="#ref-113">Brewer and Tellinghuisen, 1972</a>,  <a href="#ref-111">Tellinghuisen, 1972</a>,  <a href="#ref-112">Broyer, Vigue, et al., 1976</a>, increases smoothly from &#964;= 0.91 &#956;s at v=7
to approximately &#964;= 10 &#956;s at the highest observed levels. The measured
lifetimes  <a href="#ref-113">Brewer and Tellinghuisen, 1972</a>,  <i>missing citation</i>,  <a href="#ref-114">Capelle and Broida, 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-115">Keller, Broyer, et al., 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-116">Paisner and Wallenstein, 1974</a>,  <a href="#ref-117">Broyer, Vigue, et al., 1975</a> are considerably reduced by spontaneous predissociation
due to rotational and hyperfine mixing with B&quot; <sup>1</sup>&#928;<sub>1u</sub>, the latter leading to differences in
lifetime between ortho and para levels  <a href="#ref-112">Broyer, Vigue, et al., 1976</a>. Only near v=12 and above v~50 are the actual
lifetimes close to the purely radiative ones. The magnetic field-induced predissociation of B
<sup>3</sup>&#928;<sub>0+u</sub> was previously assumed to be caused by B' <sup>3</sup>&#928;<sub>0-u</sub>, and a potential function
for this latter state was derived from magnetic quenching data  <a href="#ref-110">Chapman and Bunker, 1972</a>,  <a href="#ref-118">Child, 1973</a>. The recent observation,
however, of a quantum interference effect between magnetic and spontaneous
predissociations  <a href="#ref-119">Broyer, Vigue, et al., 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-120">Vigue, Broyer, et al., 1974</a>,  <a href="#ref-121">Vigue, Broyer, et al., 1975</a> has established that the magnetic predissociation, too, is produced
by the B&quot; <sup>1</sup>&#928;<sub>1u</sub> state.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia24">24</a></td><td>g<sub>J</sub> varies from -0.059 at low v to -5.4<sub>5</sub> &#956;<sub>N</sub> near the dissociation limit; from Hanle
effect observations  <a href="#ref-122">Broyer and Lehmann, 1972</a>,  <a href="#ref-123">Broyer, Lehmann, et al., 1975</a>,  <a href="#ref-124">Gouedard, Broyer, et al., 1976</a>,  <a href="#ref-125">Gouedard, Broyer, et al., 1976, 2</a>. See also  <a href="#ref-126">Wallenstein, Paisner, et al., 1974</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia25">25</a></td><td>-3.3<sub>6</sub>E-7(v+1/2)<sup>2</sup> - 4.7<sub>8</sub>E-8(v+1/2)<sup>3</sup> + 3.2<sub>6</sub>E-10(v+1/2)<sup>4</sup>, from levels with
4&#8804;v&#8804;77  <a href="#ref-84">Barrow and Yee, 1973</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia26">26</a></td><td>For v &#8804; 10  <a href="#ref-84">Barrow and Yee, 1973</a>. D<sub>v</sub> increases rapidly above v=20; for more details see  <a href="#ref-101">Brown, Burns, et al., 1973</a>, <a href="#ref-85">Wei and Tellinghuisen, 1974</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia27">27</a></td><td>The continuum joining onto the discrete bands is overlapped by the B&quot;&#8592; X continuum.
A resolution of these two continua and the A &#8592;X continuum was given by  <a href="#ref-82">Tellinghuisen, 1973</a>,. See
also  <a href="#ref-127">Tellinghuisen, 1973, 2</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia28">28</a></td><td>The hyperfine structure of several lines has been observed by various high resolution laser
techiques; electric quadrupole, magnetic octupole, and other magnetic hfs constants have been
evaluated  <a href="#ref-128">Hanes and Dahlstrom, 1969</a>,  <a href="#ref-129">Kroll, 1969</a>,  <a href="#ref-130">Hansch, Levenson, et al., 1971</a>,  <a href="#ref-131">Hanes, Lapierre, et al., 1971</a>,  <a href="#ref-132">Sorem, Levenson, et al., 1971</a>,  <a href="#ref-133">Levenson and Schawlow, 1972</a>,  <a href="#ref-134">Sorem, Hansch, et al., 1972</a>,  <a href="#ref-135">Youmans, Hackel, et al., 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-136">Ruben, Kukolich, et al., 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-137">Bunker and Hanes, 1974</a>,  <i>missing citation</i>; similar analyses for <sup>129</sup>I<sub>2</sub> and
<sup>127,129</sup>I<sub>2</sub>  <a href="#ref-138">Tesic and Pao, 1975</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia29">29</a></td><td>Extrapolated from data with v' &#8805; 4. The vibrational numbering, changed  <a href="#ref-139">Steinfeld, Zare, et al., 1965</a>, by 1 from the
previous table in MOLSPEC 1, has been confirmed by isotope studies  <a href="#ref-140">Brown and James, 1965</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia30">30</a></td><td> <a href="#ref-82">Tellinghuisen, 1973</a>, suggests that the v'=4 numbering of  <a href="#ref-86">Brown, 1931</a>, may have to be raised substantially. Preliminary
results of a rotational analysis  <a href="#ref-141">Ashby, 1975</a>, of nine bands in the A &#8592;X, v&quot;=5 progression and of
three bands in the v&quot;=4 progression indicate that w' ~ 57.5  <a href="#ref-141">Ashby, 1975</a>, w'x' ~ 1.85  <a href="#ref-141">Ashby, 1975</a>, B'(for the lowest
analyzed level) = 0.02375  <a href="#ref-141">Ashby, 1975</a>, &#945;' ~ 0.0005  <a href="#ref-141">Ashby, 1975</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia31">31</a></td><td>The continuum joining onto the discrete bands has been studied by many investigators, most
recently by  <a href="#ref-82">Tellinghuisen, 1973</a>, who derives an f value of f= 0.00062  <a href="#ref-82">Tellinghuisen, 1973</a>; see also  <a href="#ref-99">Brith, Rowe, et al., 1975</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia32">32</a></td><td>Suggested as lower state of high temperature absorption bands near 3427  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>  <a href="#ref-75">Mulliken, 1971</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia33">33</a></td><td>These constants  <a href="#ref-84">Barrow and Yee, 1973</a>, represent the levels v=0-5;  <a href="#ref-85">Wei and Tellinghuisen, 1974</a>, for v=0-6, give &#969;<sub>e</sub> = 214.582, &#969;<sub>e</sub>x<sub>e</sub>
= 0.6243, B<sub>e</sub> = 0.037363, &#945;<sub>e</sub> = 0.0001145 using calculated D<sub>v</sub> values. On the basis of
the resonance series of  <a href="#ref-142">Rank and Baldwin, 1951</a>,  <a href="#ref-143">Rank and Rao, 1964</a> and  <a href="#ref-95">Verma, 1960</a>,  <a href="#ref-144">LeRoy, 1970</a> has given polynomial formulae for G(v), B<sub>v</sub>, and
D<sub>v</sub> valid up to v = 82; &#969;<sub>e</sub> = 214.548, &#969;<sub>e</sub>x<sub>e</sub> = 0.6163 , ...B<sub>e</sub> = 0.037395, &#945;<sub>e</sub> =
0.0001244, ... ,D<sub>e</sub> = 4.54E-9, &#946;<sub>e</sub> = 0.017E-9;... The most accurate constants for v = 0
were derived  <a href="#ref-145">Gerstenkorn, Luc, et al., 1977</a> from the analysis by means of Fourier transform spectroscopy of the
B&#8592;X, 30-0 bands: B<sub>0</sub> = 0.037311<sub>5</sub>, D<sub>0</sub> = 4.5<sub>5</sub>E-9, H<sub>0</sub> = -0.76E-15.
The vibrational levels of the ground state have been observed up to v = 84 [D &#8594; X
resonance series  <a href="#ref-95">Verma, 1960</a>], i.e. to within 400 cm<sup>-1</sup> of the dissociation limit. The levels v&quot; = 98...115
originally reported by  <a href="#ref-95">Verma, 1960</a>, were found to be due to an NO impurity  <a href="#ref-146">Verma and LeRoy, 1974</a>. As a consequence the
RKR potential function of  <a href="#ref-95">Verma, 1960</a>, must be corrected at high v. The RKR curves of  <a href="#ref-100">Zare, 1964</a> and  <a href="#ref-144">LeRoy, 1970</a>
extend only to v = 82 and are unaffected by this correction. <a href="#Dia35">35</a> see <a href="#Dia33">33</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia34">34</a></td><td>g<sub>J</sub>(v=0, J=12,14)=9.l3E-4 &#956;<sub>N</sub>  <a href="#ref-147">Solarz and Levy, 1972</a> from non-linear level crossing.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia35">35</a></td><td><i>missing note</i></td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia36">36</a></td><td>Raman sp. <a href="#Dia39">39</a></td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia37">37</a></td><td>From the convergence of the vibrational levels in the B <sup>3</sup>&#928;<sub>0+u</sub> state  <a href="#ref-148">Barrow, Broyd, et al., 1973</a>,  <a href="#ref-84">Barrow and Yee, 1973</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia38">38</a></td><td>Nature of the upper state (1<sub>u</sub>) and of the dissociation products confirmed by photofragment
spectroscopy  <a href="#ref-149">Clear and Wilson, 1973</a>.</td></tr>
<tr><td style="vertical-align: top;"><a id="Dia39">39</a></td><td>High resolution resonance Raman spectra of I<sub>2</sub> vapor up to the eleventh overtone (12-0).
Raman spectra in rare gas matrices  <a href="#ref-150">Howard and Andrews, 1974</a>.</td></tr>
